Can a contact sum reproduce curvature? Can an alternative
construction separate from its control? Can a coefﬁcient be
isolated from correlated terms?
The answers progressively
narrow what the successful readout must contain. They do
not make the unﬁnished portions mandatory work before the
record can be written coherently. The ﬁnished account con-
sists of the derivations, tests, failures, and boundaries already
available.
V.8 The gated CR274 readout
CR274 is the later historical applied model in the technical
spine. It starts from a ﬁtted base K, retains the exact asymme-
try structure, and adds four family-gated operators. Table V.1
gives the recorded coefﬁcients without promoting them to sub-
strate primitives.[2]
On the 55-row benchmark, the base model reports RMS
3.9102 MeV and the gated model 2.7160 MeV. Fifty of 55
rows lie within 5 MeV. The named anchors oxygen-16, iron-56,
gold-197, and lead-208 lie within 10 MeV, while ﬁve bench-
mark residual outliers remain. These statements characterize
the reported benchmark. They do not establish performance
on every isotope belonging to the same element families.
A gated model can improve a readout by allowing a shared
correction on rows satisfying a predicate. The scientiﬁc bur-
den then moves partly to the predicate: was it deﬁned inde-
pendently, was it selected after seeing residuals, and does it
retain performance outside the ﬁtted rows? The coefﬁcients re-
main empirical whether the predicate has a suggestive geomet-
ric name or not. Their proper place is downstream of source
construction, where they can be compared and replaced with-
out modifying the exact ledger.

other terms unﬁtted, and it does not retroactively make the ear-

V.9 The CR277 zero-reﬁt extension
CR277 applied the frozen CR274 base-plus-four-operator
model to a canonical 126-element surface with one represen-
tative isotope per element. It reports reproducing the earlier
model predictions at the benchmark anchors to better than
10−3 MeV, then extending the readout without reﬁtting at each
element. Table V.2 records the observed-label comparisons.[2]
The larger error in the light subset is part of the result. Those
rows lie outside the original base-ﬁt range and expose a trans-
fer limitation that would be hidden by quoting only the ear-
lier 55-row score. The 78 extended-only rows similarly isolate
rows absent from the original ﬁtted benchmark. Their RMS
is a more relevant description of that particular extension than
the training-range improvement.
“Zero reﬁt” has a precise and useful meaning here: an al-
ready ﬁtted model was applied without another ﬁt to the ex-
tension rows. It does not mean that the model had never been
ﬁtted. The distinction is parallel to the CR245 terminology
problem, but the operations are different. CR245 selects candi-
dates near ﬁtted coefﬁcients and may retain those coefﬁcients;
CR277 freezes an existing ﬁtted readout and tests its transfer.
Both can produce meaningful evidence when their histories re-
main visible.
The eight frontier rows at Z = 119 through 126 receive
sealed model outputs, not observed residuals. Their existence
in the ﬁnite periodic surface cannot be used to enlarge the mea-
sured comparison from 118 to 126 observed elements. Nor
does one representative per element establish a full isotope-
family law. The extension is a preserved historical result, su-
perseded as current binding authority by B5T but not erased by
it.
V.10 B5T: separating representation from measured bind-
ing
The current authority names B5T as a completed representa-
tion/residual test. Its central separation is temporal and infor-
mational: a tensor assignment is selected using the frozen B5R
three-dimensional Gram deformation as a target-blind locator,
then its identity is frozen before measured binding is opened.

Binding receives the resulting ordered tensor-predicted three-
dimensional path. It neither selects the tensor nor breaks a tie.
Raw Z, N, and nuclear A remain outside the stated cipher.[4]
The reported search examines all 40,320 = 8! bijections
across 3,704 unit transitions and identiﬁes one stable minimum
assignment. In pointed-role order, the assignment is
(PR00,PR01,PR02,PR03) �−→ (2,12,4,3),
(PR04,PR05,PR06,PR07) �−→ (1,6,8,9).
(V.15)
The exhaustive qualiﬁer refers to this ﬁnite assignment space.
It does not mean that every conceivable tensor representation,
geometry, or physical model has been searched. A stable mini-
mum over a deﬁned orbit is a strong ﬁnite result when its orbit
and objective are speciﬁed.

The distinction between the target-blind representation and
the subsequent binding lane is equally important. A represen-
tation can be selected without binding targets while a down-
stream predictor is still learned from development binding data.
Target-blind tensor identity does not imply that the whole resid-
ual predictor is an untrained physical law. The reported design
aims instead to prevent binding targets from choosing the rep-
resentation that will later be evaluated for binding utility.
V.11 The leakage-safe held-chain comparison
For the measured comparison, the authority reports rebuilding
the exact B4 coordinate lane with out-of-fold predictions for
1,982 development rows and the unchanged 393 held isotopes
across 17 element chains. Out-of-fold construction matters be-
cause a downstream residual model should not receive decep-
tively accurate in-sample predictions from an upstream compo-
nent. The reported held-chain split further deﬁnes the particu-
lar transfer question being tested. It does not turn the held set
into an unlimited statement about all nuclear regimes.
Table V.3 preserves every listed lane. The tensor principal
has the lowest reported RMSE among them. Its improvement
over the direct-3D comparison is about 0.008 MeV in this met-
ric, and over the external-coordinate baseline about 0.027 MeV.
The numerical differences are useful precisely at that scale;
they need not be described as a qualitative solution of binding
to be meaningful.
The count within 1 MeV answers a different question from
RMSE. RMSE squares each residual and therefore responds
strongly to larger errors; the threshold count treats all rows
inside the chosen interval alike. The inconsistent-channel re-
label control also has 348 rows within 1 MeV, yet a higher
RMSE. Thus the principal’s advantage is not that it uniquely
maximizes every possible score. It wins the stated RMSE com-
parisons while sharing one threshold count. Preserving both

V.12 The direction-erased diagnostic and the current
boundary
The B5T authority also retains an important unfavorable com-
parison for the selected directed construction.
A direction-
erased diagnostic reaches relative RMSE 0.931302002638, be-
low the directed locator’s 0.990887476018. This diagnostic
was not a frozen T threshold and therefore does not change the
reported classiﬁcation. It nevertheless limits how the selected
directional representation should be interpreted.
A diagnostic can expose structure that the formal pass con-
ditions did not require the test to optimize. Here it indicates
that endpoint sign and role/tensor identity may not be most ef-
fectively represented by the selected joint construction. That
observation is an existing result, not a license to replace the
frozen principal after seeing the outcome. The source recom-
mends a future target-blind factorization route, while explicitly
saying it is not yet mapped, owner-conﬁrmed, or authorized for
execution. This volume records that boundary without turning
it into a new campaign.
B5T installs an informational three-dimensional tensor as-
signment. The current authority explicitly does not install a
physical tensor, nuclear geometry, isotope-local tensor, A/qA
27

SAM Volume II | Chapter V
Brady | 10 November 2026
conversion, SI-energy law, Higgs law, or physical W9P cross-
walk. It is not B6 and not a ﬁnal binding formula. These limits
describe which bridge remains absent: numerical and represen-
tational usefulness has not yet been converted into a physical
account of where nuclear energy resides or how the conﬁgura-
tion produces it.
The chapter can therefore end at a completed result rather
than an unﬁnished obligation. The exact source identity is es-
tablished conditionally. Historical ﬁt and transfer results are
preserved with their empirical inputs. B5T supplies the re-
tained representation and held-residual benchmark. None re-
quires the author to begin another search in order to state accu-
rately what has already been done.
V.13 The record after B5T, by contract
The B5T authority of 16 August 2026 was not the end of the
binding record. Between then and October the archive’s bind-
ing and exchange authorities carried every later readout, and
this section puts them on one page with the single rule that
makes them readable: each number belongs to a contract, a ros-
ter, a reference convention, a training lane and a holdout rule,
and numbers from different contracts are not ranked against
each other. The archive states the rule itself for the strongest
case: “B5T393’s 0.940107903 MeV and 348 within 1 MeV
use a separate roster/evaluation contract.”[152, 155]
Contract A: B5T’s holdout.
1,982 development rows, 393
held isotopes from 17 whole element chains, the B4 coor-
dinate baseline, held RMSE in MeV. Every successor on
this contract lost to B5T and was recorded as a falsiﬁca-
tion: the A3D40 feature graft (ﬁve feature bodies, thirty de-
velopment models; held 0.942385875), the B6 architecture
sweep (3,168 candidates, seventy improving development out-
of-fold, none of the 24 frozen survivors beating the holdout;
best 0.9424625894, within-1 count falling from 348 to 345),
the semantic-ﬁber sweep’s best sealed additive construction
(held RMSE 61.95), and the readout-correct successor (held
max/MAE/RMSE 7.724/3.848/4.431, better than its own ad-
ditive control, worse than B5T). After B6 the authority wrote
that same-class append-and-lift searches were stopped and that
a successor “must change the binding action or target con-
struction itself.”[152] The one lead it names and that never
received its own campaign is the direction-erased locator of
Section V.12.

belongs to Parts II and III: its fourteen features are cohesive
energy per nucleon in the current and literal channels, spin
contact means, relative history and the neutron-rule cell depth,
which are the objects of Chapters VII–IX, XI, XII and XVIII.
Binding comes out of it as the paid work of a retained source,
which is also Volume I’s accumulation and Volume III’s recip-
rocal history in another register. The source model is the one
that speaks the volume’s language, and from this edition it is
written as the volume’s model. B5T remains the benchmark
and the grammar’s one representation tested against binding
target-blind.
Two host computations of 25 October 2026 on the recorded
predictions, with no ﬁt and no new data, ﬁx the relation be-
tween them.[157, 158] First, the 253-isotope contract was
carved from B5T’s roster: its 208 development rows are all
B5T development rows and its 32 historical rows are all B5T
held rows. On those same isotopes B5T is the better predic-
tor on both splits (development 1.935 against 2.048 MeV, 141
against 72 rows within 1 MeV; held 1.064 against 1.671, 30
against 13), and most of that margin is the B4 coordinate base-
line trained on 1,982 rows rather than B5T’s own path features
(held 1.064 against 1.112 for the baseline alone). Second, the
light isotopes were outside every tensor roster for one reason
only: B4’s roster rule of August admitted Z ≥ 8, so none of
H, He, Li, Be, B, C or N ever had a path. That was a design
choice in the record, not a direction of the owner.
The source model has no such limit, and the second compu-
tation moved the light rows into its folds. The recorded model
was ﬁrst reproduced exactly (whole binding as target, fourteen
features, no constant term, folds 0–4: full-ﬁt and out-of-fold
predictions identical on all 253 rows). Then the thirteen light
and anchor rows were placed inside the same ﬁve folds and the
model reﬁt by exact rational least squares, with the prediction
written before running that the light error would fall under 3
MeV, the development error worsen by under 0.1 MeV and the
free constituents stay several MeV off. All three held.
From Li-6 upward the light rows now sit at the heavy rows’
error: Li-6 +1.0, Li-7 −0.2, Be-9 +1.1, B-10 +1.9, B-11
+0.8, C-12 −1.5, C-13 −2.2, N-14 −3.0, N-15 −2.3 MeV out
of fold. He-4 stays 4 MeV off, and the three free constituents
cannot be carried by this linear source model at any weight-
ing (7 to 14 MeV either way). The light-source and fragment
ledgers identify the missing source/action/reference account at
these endpoints. The revised Chapters XI, XII and XVI de-
velop that account through conditional complete-energy com-
parisons and speciﬁed formation mechanisms. The empirical
reﬁt here remains a separate historical comparison.
Two things remain undone and are stated as such. The tensor
channel still has no value below Z = 8; giving the light chains
a path means deﬁning it for Z < 8 with the frozen assignment
of Eq. (V.15), a change to B5T’s construction and the owner’s
decision. And the two models have not been joined: whether
B5T’s eight role values say anything in source-cell terms is
the Θ18 question of Chapter I in another form, and no run has
asked it.
V.15 A binding claim with its inputs still visible
The strongest coherent statement supported by this chain has
several parts. The four-slot source construction gives a signed
neutron-excess gap. Its normalized square yields the asymme-
try shape with an exact rational factor. A full readout requires
additional size, charge, parity, and family dependencies; histor-
ical attempts demonstrate that linear contact accounting and
direct source-to-mass conversion do not sufﬁce. Fitted gates
improve a stated benchmark but lose accuracy on some zero-
reﬁt extensions. B5T improves the held-chain residual on its
own contract relative to every listed comparison and every later
challenger, while leaving physical interpretation open. The
spin and source-cell model, the volume’s own, carries every
isotope from Li-6 upward at about 2 MeV once the light rows
are inside it, and fails on the free constituents where the refer-
ence is a convention.
These are compatible ﬁndings, not successive claims that
earlier limitations have vanished.
The same exact algebra
can support several empirical readouts.
A better residual
model can coexist with an incomplete physical mechanism. A
failed historical shortcut can remain valuable after the current
method changes. Two models with different languages can
both stand in the record until a construction joins them. The
manuscript’s task is to carry those distinctions intact across the
transition from mathematical construction to measurement.
The dedicated spin section next develops exact interacting
sources, thermal preparation, hidden memory and physical re-
ceiving response.
The nuclear chapters that follow receive
those methods through the actual isotope action and ordered
contact history. Element families, representative isotopes, and
composites can be organized using the ﬁnite framework while
retaining separate questions about stability, binding, and chem-
istry. That separation permits the volume to be complete as an
account of its results while carrying the remaining physical as-
signments explicitly.

SAM VOLUME II
Exact spin sources and ﬁnite thermal structure
Chapter VI
VI.1 What the spin construction contributes to matter
The spin program supplies a family of ﬁnite interacting sources
with a complete account of their states.
Its contribution to
matter construction starts at that level. A speciﬁed source has
an interaction, an energy convention, retained boundary coor-
dinates, a preparation and a receiving observable. Complete
spectra and thermal factors then calculate how internal states
contribute to a ﬁnite response.
The later nuclear construc-
tions receive this method through their actual contact phases
and source actions. Their connection is established by those
maps, rather than by counting every binary coordinate as an
additional material constituent.
This dedicated section develops the completed spin work in
ﬁve steps. The present chapter deﬁnes the exact sources and
their thermal organization. Chapter VII follows a speciﬁed
microscopic source through hidden memory and ordered read-
ings. Chapter VIII develops composition control and its kinetic
response. Chapter IX brings the complete physical response
bounds through the latest source Dirichlet result. Chapter X
maps the resulting objects into the surrounding volumes. The
organization is editorial synthesis; each source result retains its
original model, numerical scope and classiﬁcation.[99]
The distinction between sources matters from the beginning.
The dense signed packet systems, the Kac-normalized compo-
sition sources, the all-temperature hidden-spin compiler and
the actual isotope phase encodings have different Hamiltoni-
ans and clocks. They share exact counting, conditional prepa-
ration and retained-history methods. The section states which
source is acting before using its results.

Varying the correction-free fraction gives exact tricritical

The recorded pair control with a vanishing quartic term and a

classify the transition. The ratio result carries the classiﬁcation:
The test result suggests strong contact with the concept.[104]

SAM VOLUME II
Microscopic Spin Sources and the Memory of an Accepted History
Chapter VII
VII.1 An interacting interior behind a ﬁnite boundary
The frustrated-spin program gives Volume II a complete exam-
ple of how a small matter-facing boundary can retain a large,
active interior. Pairwise microscopic interactions generate col-
lective boundary terms. Exact thermal elimination determines
the boundary equilibrium, while the microscopic generator de-
termines what happens between observations. Those opera-
tions meet in one source without becoming the same operation.
The completed work then carries the actual interior through
accepted readings, constructs reusable response maps, and en-
closes very long histories with their survival probability and
complete fast remainder.
The exposition here synthesizes the completed source
derivations of the compiler, conditional-memory certiﬁcates
and source-bound consumers. This chapter develops that mi-
croscopic line. The nuclear chapters that follow apply spin
representations and memory methods to their own actual iso-
tope sources.
VII.2 Exact thermal elimination and programmable inter-
actions
Take four boundary signs s = (s1,s2,s3,s4) and a hidden sign h.
A full-port star of coupling J and sign row p = (1, p2, p3, p4)
has energy −Jh(p·s). Summing the hidden sign gives
zJ,p(s) = 2cosh[βJ(p·s)].
For independent copies with nonnegative integer multiplicities
wp, the conditional partition functions multiply and their free
energies add:
Zs = ∏
p
zJ,p(s)wp,
F(s) = −β −1 logZs.
The constant part of F remains part of absolute free energy,
although it cancels from normalized boundary probabilities.
[111][112]
Expand in boundary characters χA(s) = ∏i∈A si. A single
zero-ﬁeld star has one constant, six pair terms and a quartet.
Its nonconstant scalar coefﬁcients are
pJ = −logcosh(4βJ)
8β

qJ = 4logcosh(2βJ)−logcosh(4βJ)

Sign copies multiply each coefﬁcient by TA = ∑p wpχA(p).
This gives a ﬁnite interaction compiler: choose the surviving
character transforms, invert them to nonnegative copy counts,
and retain the resulting microscopic source.
The completed selective compiler establishes why subgroup
projection alone has a particular support. Averaging over a
sign subgroup retains its annihilator, which is closed under
character multiplication. Retaining s1s2 and s3s4 therefore also
permits their quartet; retaining s1s2 and s1s3 permits s2s3. Un-
equal nonnegative copies can remove those companion terms.
Within the ﬁxed eight full-port unit-star dictionary, the exact
minimum counts for a pure quartet, two disjoint pairs, two
overlapping pairs, and two disjoint pairs plus their quartet are
respectively 4,8,8,2. Local two-port modules give the two-
pair supports with two hidden spins. These constructions com-
pare declared supports and preserve their actual coefﬁcient
relations.[112]
The integer compiler makes its count statement explicit. For
a primitive integer target transform vector vA, put

uniform layer: it changes the interior free energy while leaving
normalized boundary probabilities unchanged. The completed
hidden-boundary work also gives connected, nonisomorphic
interiors with the same uniform boundary state at every temper-
ature, and explicit hidden interventions that distinguish them.
A complete normalized boundary equilibrium therefore need
not identify its interior source.[111][113]
VII.3 The all-temperature ratio-one source
Different-order coefﬁcients in one coupling layer retain differ-
ent temperature dependence. The two-scale construction sup-
plies the exact identity
q2J = p2J −4pJ.
Its ratio-one source uses pair/quartet transform units (4,0) at J
and (−1,−1) at 2J. The resulting coefﬁcients satisfy
P = 4(4pJ − p2J) = −4q2J = Q < 0,
so the complete boundary free energy is
F(s) = C(β)+P(β)s1s2(1+s3s4).
One parity sector of the second pair disables the ﬁrst pair’s ef-
fective interaction; the other doubles it. The ratio Q/P = 1
holds for every β,J > 0, while the strengths themselves vary
with beta. The derivation follows directly from the two log-
cosh functions; the positivity of q2J keeps the denominator
nonzero. [114][115]

SAM VOLUME II
Composition, thermal cancellation and intrinsic spin memory
Chapter VIII
VIII.1 Which changes alter a response, and which change
only its clock?
A spin source can keep the same visible inventory while chang-
ing the hidden preparation that supplies its next response. The
completed composition sequence gives that statement a de-
tailed mechanism. Exact component factors turn a change in
graph composition into a covariance under the full thermal
ﬁeld. A particular integer change cancels its ﬁrst ﬁeld term.
A unique moment condition removes the next term, and two
derived temperature corrections suppress the complete count
response further. At sixth order a new hidden interaction sur-
vives every shared thermal correction. Its rational dependence
on relaxation scale identiﬁes the hidden directions that a re-
ceiver detects.[68][69][72]
The sequence uses the Kac-normalized pair/triangle/free-
spin family introduced in Chapter VI, its literal ﬁfteen-spin
root and the original reading interval θ = 1/3.
A comple-
mentary kinetic calculation uses a distinct four-boundary/24-
hidden-spin source.
There the microscopic boundary ﬂip
keeps the actual hidden coordinates; replacing it by a thermal-
boundary ﬂip changes individual edge rates. The completed
comparison determines exactly when that change could be a
common clock multiplier, and ﬁnds that the retained source
does not satisfy the condition. These two constructions meet
at the source-to-receiver interface while retaining their differ-
ent Hamiltonians and clocks.[73][74]
The Hamiltonians, component inventories, preparations and
readouts are model assignments inherited from their original
sources. The covariance identities, cancellation rules, sixth-
order decomposition and kinetic comparison below are com-
pleted deductions from those assignments.
The completed
source campaigns classify their results as: The test result sug-
gests strong contact with the concept.[77][78][79][80][74]

cancellation in one weight, one rate or one forcing vector.[75]
The two memory channels have complementary operational
meanings. The between-reading quantity compares the actual
ordered three-reading return with the product formed from its
one-interval return. The within-interval quantity compares that
one-interval return product with the constant-rate expression
using the actual source mean. The two differences retain their
signs. Neither is replaced by the variance of a rate distribu-
tion. Their composition derivatives can vanish at a selected
order even when the underlying memory observables remain
nonzero. In the feedback derivative, the second term above is
just as necessary as the forcing derivative: changing composi-
tion changes the operator that propagates hidden information
as well as the information injected into it.[75]
The center subtraction has a similarly explicit role. Each
complete thermal quantity compares the integrated source re-
sponse with the source-center response at the same tempera-
ture. A count change at ﬁxed temperature and center has zero
derivative on the center side.
Its complete center value re-
mains stored, while the count susceptibility comes from the
integrated source. Along a changing-temperature path the cen-
ter has its own tangent. The corrected families below always
evaluate the count derivative at their corrected ﬁxed tempera-
ture, preserving that distinction between selecting a source and
differentiating an observable on it.[75]

response.[72]
The remainder construction explains the strength and scope
of that statement. On a ﬁxed small-X neighborhood the an-
alytic branch, root and logarithmic factors have uniformly
bounded derivatives.
The normalized ﬁeld expansion is an
expansion in polynomially weighted integrals over the whole
half-line. A small growing-U split controls exponential Tay-
lor remainders, while quartic decay pays the complementary
range. Outside that neighborhood the exact negative fourth-
derivative bound controls the ﬁeld; beyond the retained far-
ﬁeld threshold the original Gaussian bound is used. The count
covariance supplies an additional factor of δ from the noncon-
stant root, converting the score remainder into O(δ 7) in the re-
sponse. The full protocol’s analytic Hessian and cubic deriva-
tive carry the same paid error through preparation, relay and
resolvent.[72]
For ﬁnite sources the record also retains non-asymptotic
ﬁeld costs. If G is the actual score oscillation and Cﬁeld its
complete ﬁeld bound, then the mean derivative is paid by
G/2. Consequently the invariant satisﬁes both the sum of its
separately evaluated feedback and mean costs and the gen-
eral bound |J(1)| ≤ (302/27)Cﬁeld. These remain available
even at a size where the degree-seven correction is signiﬁ-
cant. At s = 4, for example, the displayed J/δ 6 at N = 1016
is about 2.02987×10−3, while its limiting coefﬁcient is about
5.62137 × 10−5. The ﬁnite correction is retained rather than
hidden by the limiting positive law.[72][80]

SAM VOLUME II
The Physical Spin Source and Its Complete Response Receiver
Chapter IX
IX.1 A retained source becomes a quantitative receiver
The microscopic and composition constructions supply a
frustrated-spin source whose visible ports retain a much larger
interacting state. Its physical receiver program asks a concrete
next question: how much of an original port difference sur-
vives a speciﬁed history of actual site attempts? The completed
work answers with source-derived response intervals, explicit
hidden directions and paid reﬁnements of the original ﬁnite-
system dynamics. Every improvement retains the same Gibbs
preparation, all source sites, signed couplings and original at-
tempt clock.
There are eighteen original families, each with BASE,
H_MINUS and H_PLUS preparations.
Each of these ﬁfty-
four preparations carries thirty-seven histories, from N/1000
through 1000N site attempts, giving 1998 source/history cases.
The preparations are distinct Gibbs measures with their orig-
inal integer inventories. Their inherited count exchanges pre-
serve N, inverse temperature, short-source parameters and cen-
ter.
In particular, the exchange ∆(P,T,F) = (−12,+7,+3)
preserves the number of sites because 2(−12) + 3(7) + 3 = 0.
Common family parameters permit selected source kernels to
be shared; preparation moments and coefﬁcients remain at-
tached to their own measure. [130][132]
This chapter is an editorial synthesis of completed source
calculations and their derivations.
Their completed source
and receiver results carry the classiﬁcation The test result sug-
gests strong contact with the concept. The account follows
the mathematical mechanism through its successive completed
operands, including the current paid source-Dirichlet result.

natively: a clock recipient rescales time and leaves every stage
order invisible, by identity recipe on every stage state; an en-
ergy recipient changes the record’s law and is order-sensitive,
with a lapse term that at gain-optimal ﬁelds varies by a factor of
four across ﬁeld families while the gains agree to 1.8 × 10−6.
Every chain’s continuous total optimum was certiﬁed a local
maximum natively, the ﬁeld and lapse ledgers failing the same
cross with equal and opposite gradients, which is the mecha-
nism behind that dependence. The switched share at lapse 1/4
exceeds the share at 1/2 on every family, witnessed.
The lawfulness gate built for Volume I’s line of sight (realisa-
tion, monotonicity, slope) was run on this chapter’s objects the
same day. The one-stage gain g(d) is neither a positive mix-
ture nor a one-signed relaxation (g − g∞ changes sign). The
hidden spin’s autocorrelation is a positive two-mode mixture,
and its ﬁrst ﬂip is a positive mixture from a Gibbs entrance
and a hidden-stage ledger from the ground entrance, with the
threshold 4/5 < p∗ < 5/6 at β = 1: the label belongs to the en-

{0,−1,−(1 ∓ |ρ|)/2}, the hazard sum ﬁxed by the attempt
clock and split by the Gibbs correlation with a closed form for
|ρ(µ)|, the −1 mode the parity, weightless. “Hazard as tem-

XI.17 What the successful relaxation changes
Exact bounds on the earlier trial spaces explain why simply
repeating the same construction was insufﬁcient. The physi-
cal125 family has V ≤ 19/3, the original600 source has V ≤
47/7, and the complete925 kinetic/current closure has V ≤ 7.
In their stated coherent-ﬁeld trial families these bounds miss
the relevant supplied channel lower.
The successful maps
reach the physical complement while preserving source infor-
mation. An inﬁnite ﬁlter, by contrast, can discard source direc-
tions; a ﬁnite ﬁlter with one polar normalization retains them
with a paid residual. [171]
The comparison is between a full energy upper and proper
matter-particle fragment lowers. Translation ﬁbers, escaping
free bosons and a cluster identiﬁcation of the essential spec-
trum require their own spectral argument. Atomic endpoints
additionally require the electron and recoil recipients devel-
oped later in this volume. The immediate next layer is con-
structive: which controls create the certiﬁed state, what source
arrives, and where the energy and information go during prepa-
ration?

XII.1 A visible source value does not close its next response
The completed nuclear construction carries more than a min-
imum energy or instantaneous current. A preparation can re-
tain phase information invisible to the current receiver, while a
changed source leaves a propagating ﬁeld that affects later con-
tacts. These two forms of memory have explicit source maps.
This chapter connects their exact ﬁnite construction to the de-
rived successive-contact process on the full phase and wave
state. The cited campaigns distinguish their inherited operands,
derivations and completed numerical panels.
For the actual Li6 source at ρ = 1, write

The retained h enters the next visible response. Composing
only the visible compressions FUeR removes that contribution
between Writes. [31]

The Li-6 receiver algebra of this chapter and Chapter XVIII

New by four separate routes:
the two preparations are one frame under the complex struc-

XII.10 From a retained state to a formation protocol
The certiﬁcate in Chapter XI supplies a complete target en-
ergy for the unchanged Li6 contact preparation. A formation
protocol additionally speciﬁes its incoming state, allowed con-
trols, time dependence, work store and output records. The
receiving-action continuation makes these ingredients explicit.
The results in this section are conditional analytical construc-
tions with ﬁnite exact and numerical checks; they have not
acquired the independent CR2 status of the preceding energy
certiﬁcate.[176]
Three preparations occur in the discussion and should re-
main distinct. The certiﬁed contact input is F0P9z14336, with
its actual Pauli metric, initially tensored with scalar vacuum.

cell and the residual pair in another. An asymptotically pre-

and preparation records. The following accounts start with one
of the ﬁrst two speciﬁed states. Replacing them by the third re-
quires those additional source and energy operands.

SAM VOLUME II
Elements, isotopes and composite matter
Chapter XIII
XIII.1 From a nuclear address to a periodic family
The nuclear ledger distinguishes proton number, neutron num-
ber and the channels carried by their populations. A periodic
construction asks a different question: how should the ﬁnite
matter account organize families whose members share a pro-
ton number? The move from an isotope to an element family
removes information for a particular purpose. It does not turn
every isotope of the family into the same nuclear state.
The current source construction assigns one native family
row to each integer Z from 1 through 126. It audits the native
coordinate before applying a familiar name or measured com-
parator. The order is important because an externally supplied
label cannot count as a prediction of a construction that used
the label to choose its own input. In this account the native row
is constructed ﬁrst; the label is a downstream ﬁeld.[1, 3]
The ﬁnite periodic surface is useful as an organized model
inventory. It places known-label families and source-native
frontier families within one declared indexing scheme.
Its
value does not depend on calling every row an observed ele-
ment. The scheme can be exact as an inventory while the phys-
ical status of an individual frontier row remains unestablished.
The distinction is carried explicitly through this chapter.
Chemistry belongs to this volume because elements, iso-
topes and composites continue the matter construction. This
placement does not imply that a complete molecular chemistry
operator has been derived. This chapter supplies the family in-
dexing, nuclear populations and source-support channels. The
later chapters then develop the completed occupied atomic and
molecular source constructions, including full pair ﬁelds, col-
lective recoil and paid motion. The family inventory and the
dynamical calculation retain their own deﬁnitions.
XIII.2 Three row types and two maps
An element-family row is denoted EZ. An isotope row has the
more informative address
IZ,N = (Z,N,Anuc = Z +N,...),
(XIII.1)
where the remaining ﬁelds are deﬁned by the source schema.
The family projection is
πZ(IZ,N) = EZ.
(XIII.2)
This map is many-to-one whenever the retained isotope ros-
ter contains more than one neutron count for the same proton
count. The information it discards is not invalid information.
It is simply not needed for the grouping question answered by
the periodic surface.
A representative-isotope table requires another map,
σrep(EZ) = IZ,Nrep(Z).
(XIII.3)
The selector supplies one isotope for a one-row-per-element
comparison. It does not invert the family projection on the en-
tire isotope roster. Nor does it assert that the chosen isotope is
the only allowed, stable or physically important member of the
family. Those statements would require additional predicates.
The distinction can be followed directly in a readout. First
construct EZ. Then record the selector that supplies Nrep(Z).
Next evaluate the nuclear or binding operator on the selected
(Z,N) pair. Finally compare the resulting ﬁeld with a measured
value if the row has an authorized comparator. If the selected
isotope changes, the family coordinate may remain ﬁxed while
the readout changes. The selector is therefore part of the com-
parison speciﬁcation.
This separation also explains why a periodic roster and an
isotope vault can have different sizes without disagreement.
The LC05 account retains 126 native family rows and an older
scoped roster of 162 isotope rows. Neither number describes
the cardinality of every possible isotope family. The ﬁrst is the
declared range of the periodic construction. The second is a
particular retained source roster. Their coexistence is expected
once the row types are stated.
XIII.3 Native construction before label reveal
For each permitted coordinate the source emits a row of the
form
EZ = (Z,native_idZ,frontierZ,...).
(XIII.4)
The ellipsis is not permission to ﬁll absent physical columns
by analogy. It represents only ﬁelds already supplied by the
source construction. In particular, a native identiﬁer does not
supply a shell conﬁguration, a half-life, a decay channel or a
molecular interaction law.
The periodic invariant audit checks that the coordinate lies
in the declared range, that the number of rows is 126, and that
the coordinates are unique. After that audit the label stage sup-
plies 118 known labels for Z ≤ 118 and eight native frontier
identiﬁers for 119 ≤ Z ≤ 126. These are descriptions of the
source table’s reveal policy. A successful label join validates
the correspondence it actually checks; it does not by itself
establish every physical property associated with the conven-
tional name.
The chronological order prevents label-ﬁrst backﬁlling. In
a label-ﬁrst procedure, a familiar name could select an isotope
and the isotope could then be used to construct the row claimed
to explain that name. That would make a visually convinc-
ing table without preserving the direction of inference. The re-
tained procedure puts the invariant audit before reveal so that
the native inventory can be inspected without relying on the
display labels.
This does not make the full construction independent of all
historical discovery. A method can be native-ﬁrst in its exe-
73

SAM Volume II | Chapter XIII
Brady | 10 November 2026
cuted replay while its design arose from earlier examination
of familiar data. The stronger claim of prospective empirical
prediction requires a corresponding frozen design and an eligi-
ble unseen comparison. The source order is a necessary piece
of that discipline, not a substitute for the complete history of
discovery and testing.
XIII.4 The two surfaces of cardinality 126
The retained matter capacity is M = 126, and two major source
surfaces share that count. Their partitions are different. The
ﬁnite matter inventory is organized as
126 = 63 single+63 bound,
(XIII.5)
while the periodic surface is organized as
126 = 118 known labels+8 frontier.
(XIII.6)
These decompositions make clear why equal cardinality can-
not be used as an elementwise identity. The membership pred-
icates differ, and the rows carry different kinds of addresses.
A row-position pairing between the two tables would cer-
tainly be possible as a display operation: ﬁrst row with ﬁrst
row, second with second, and so on. Such a pairing is not a
source-derived physical relation. To give it scientiﬁc meaning,
the pairing would need to preserve a declared property or fol-
low an independently speciﬁed map. The mere existence of a
bijection between ﬁnite sets of equal size is insufﬁcient.
The source account calls their common value a structural
contact at the retained horizon. That statement can be retained
without overextending it. The construction organizes both sur-
faces using a common ﬁnite capacity while allowing them to
answer different questions. Keeping two schemas is not an ad-
mission of numerical inconsistency. It is what permits their
respective information to remain available.
The same reasoning applies to other totals in the volume.
The 321-row catalog, its 169 bound entries, the 80-row stable
structural surface, and the 100-row computation grammar are
not rival answers to one census question. They are different
selected views. A periodic-family reader need not regard ev-
ery carrier or rejected catalog candidate as an element, and an
executable grammar need not contain a separate operation for
every isotope represented in an input table.
XIII.5 The reported periodic replay
The LC05 source replay reports retention of all 126 native pe-
riodic rows, the 118 downstream labels, eight native frontier
identities, the scoped 162-row isotope roster and the frontier
seal inherited from CR071.
Its decimal replay of the one-
eighth/seven-eighths source split reports maximum error 10−96
against a 10−90 tolerance. These are recorded replay quantities,
not a new execution undertaken for this manuscript.[3]
The diagnostic alternatives include label backﬁll, row dele-
tion, primitive mutation and frontier relabeling.
Each chal-
lenges a speciﬁc part of the construction. Deleting a row chal-
lenges the declared inventory; changing a primitive challenges
dependence on the locked structural inputs; replacing a frontier
status with a known label challenges the distinction between

A numerical tolerance in a decimal implementation is com-
patible with exact underlying rational identities.
It records
the implementation’s arithmetic behavior. It does not enlarge
the physical meaning of the identities.
A very small split-
reconstruction error is evidence about the split calculation, not
evidence that a frontier nucleus has been synthesized or that its
chemical behavior follows from the same tolerance.
The appropriate result is therefore a ﬁnite periodic and iso-
tope record with reproducible labels and source custody. The
completed replay establishes this inventory. The occupied elec-
tronic and molecular constructions developed later in this edi-
tion provide the subsequent dynamical recipients.
XIII.6 The balanced frontier addresses
The eight source-native frontier families use balanced anchors
N = Z,
Anuc = 2Z.
(XIII.7)
Their names are internal native identiﬁers of the SAM construc-
tion. They are retained here to make the source record identiﬁ-
able, without presenting them as recognized observations.
At the terminal address,
Z = N = 126 = M.
(XIII.8)
It follows that
Anuc = 252 = 2M = 14Θ.
(XIII.9)
The balanced nuclear ledger gives
u = 2Z +N = 378,
d = Z +2N = 378,
(XIII.10)
and the neutral electron count is e = Z = 126. With the source
volume address ˆV = 54,
u = d = 7 ˆV.
(XIII.11)
Every equality in this chain follows from the displayed deﬁni-
tions. The chain does not require an empirical measurement of
the frontier row in order to be an exact internal identity.
CR273 records a ﬁve-way terminus contact involving the
matter horizon, the source doubly-magic predicate, carrier-
mode inheritance, noble-cipher inheritance and component
74

symmetry. These descriptions refer to the source predicates. A
familiar phrase such as “doubly magic” or “noble” must not be
allowed to import an unstated experimental nuclear or chem-
ical conclusion. The arithmetic and model predicates remain
available for inspection; observation, stability and chemical be-
havior remain separate statuses.
The balanced choice also has a clear domain. It supplies a
particular anchor for each frontier family. It does not establish
that the balanced isotope is the physically realized or longest-
lived isotope, nor that all other neutron counts are excluded.
The family and the anchor continue to be different objects even
when the table displays one row per family.
XIII.7 Historical readout and zero-reﬁt extension
The historical nuclear readout enters after the nuclear ledger
and asymmetry columns have been constructed. CR274 re-
ports a 55-row ﬁtted benchmark. The base readout has RMS
3.9102 MeV. Four ﬁtted family gates reduce the reported RMS
to 2.7160 MeV, with 50 of 55 rows within 5 MeV. The ﬁtted
gates are parameters of that empirical construction. They do
not become substrate primitives merely because the resulting
expression uses structural coordinates.[3]
CR277 then freezes that operator and extends it to one repre-
sentative isotope per Z. It ﬁrst reproduces the retained bench-
mark predictions to less than 10−3 MeV and then evaluates
the extended table without reﬁtting. The sequence is impor-
tant: construction and ﬁtting precede freezing; benchmark re-
production checks the implemented frozen operator; extension
examines its behavior on a broader representative surface.
The reported observed-label subset contains 118 rows and
has RMS 6.672 MeV. Its light subset, Z = 1 through 7, has
seven rows and RMS 19.907 MeV. The middle subset, Z =
8 through 82, has 75 rows and RMS 4.145 MeV. The heavy
subset, Z = 83 through 118, has 36 rows and RMS 5.752 MeV.
The extended-only comparison has 78 rows and RMS 7.974
MeV.
These ﬁgures should be read together. The full-table resid-
ual is not represented adequately by the more favorable mid-
dle subset, and the light-row miss should not disappear when
a global value is quoted. The zero-reﬁt extension provides in-
formation precisely because the operator was not adjusted to
absorb every new deviation. It reveals a domain in which the
frozen readout transfers less well.
Zero reﬁt is a statement about the extension, not the origin
of the coefﬁcients. It does not convert the historical ﬁtted op-
erator into a wholly parameter-free law. Nor does it turn a
representative-isotope evaluation into a test over all isotopes
of all element families. The frozen readout, the representative
selector, the comparator source and the reported residual must
remain joined in the account.
This historical chain is also distinct from the later current
B5T binding authority discussed in the preceding chapter. The
tests have different representations, data splits and questions.
Their RMS values should not be ranked as though they were
interchangeable scores from one benchmark. The historical
periodic readout remains evidence about that construction’s
extension; B5T remains a later representation and held-chain
residual result with its own physical limits.
XIII.8 What makes a composite more than a collection
A collection of admitted constituents is the beginning of a com-
posite description, not its completion. A composite also re-
quires a connector pattern, a debit or credit operator, a closure
gate and a source-emission rule. The source abstraction is
C =
�
{ri},Γ,D,Gclose,Esource
�
.
(XIII.12)
The constituent set records which rows are present. The con-
nector pattern Γ records how they are related. The operator D
speciﬁes the relevant debit or credit. The gate decides whether
that connected conﬁguration closes. The emission rule states
how an admitted composite returns support.
Adding catalog indices cannot recover these ﬁelds. Indices
are identiﬁers, and changing the order of a table can change an
index without changing the physical or grammatical object it
identiﬁes. Even if an index happened to equal a useful scalar,
the sum of indices would still omit orientation, pair class, gate
state and emitted support. The composite construction must
preserve the relationships that make the collection one object.
This explains the different composite counts encountered
earlier. CR252 retains 169 bound entries inside the 321-row
catalog. LC06 retains 63 bound writes inside a 126-row mat-
ter inventory. A four-slot nucleus is yet another representation.
The entries are selected and grouped under different predicates.
A complete account can preserve all three without manufactur-
ing a universal row-number correspondence.
The four-slot ledger supplies constituent population and
channel information. It does not, by itself, specify every con-
nector of a three-dimensional object. A graph-based reception
construction may add exact incidence for a particular object,
while an empirical binding operator supplies a separate energy
readout. The fact that these descriptions can be joined at se-
lected interfaces does not make each description independently
sufﬁcient for all the others.
XIII.9 From periodic families to occupied and moving mat-
ter
The periodic construction supplies family coordinates, labels,
neutral electron counts and isotope grouping. The complete
nuclear source adds conditional states and histories. The next
step receives that source in an occupied electronic system.
The following chapters develop the completed scalar and
polarization Hamiltonian, Pauli occupation, direct and ex-
change pairs, whole ion/free references and collective recoil.
Their molecular continuation retains all 91 carbon and 153
oxygen moving reference channels, source-sector kinetic mo-
tion and paid virtual binding witnesses.
These dynamical
constructions receive the inventory and supply the additional
state and interaction data. Reaction rates, resolved molecu-
lar spectra and environmental response retain their separate
observables.[12][27]

SAM VOLUME II
Occupied Atoms from the Complete Nuclear Source
Chapter XIV
XIV.1 From a neutral count to an electronic state
A neutral ledger speciﬁes an electron inventory, Ne = Z. To
construct an atom one must also specify the electronic space,
its kinetic action, the ﬁeld to which it couples, its antisymme-
try, and the energy of the separated constituents. The com-
pleted Volume II work supplies these objects on the original
helium and carbon sources. The transition is substantial: the
electron entry becomes an occupied state with orbital coefﬁ-
cients, spin labels, radial moments, a complete Hamiltonian
and an explicit ionization reference. Nuclear phase multiplic-
ity remains part of the object throughout.
The helium inputs contain 2p2n and all 4096 original phase
histories per cover. The carbon inputs contain 6p6n and all
68,719,476,736 = 236 original histories. Their intrinsic ener-
gies and signed receiver U are retained. At the ground-state
level these sources have 49 helium conditional receiver sectors
and 361 ordered carbon sectors. A shared electron source acts
on each of these original nuclear states before the nuclear mini-
mum or Gibbs average is taken. The construction consequently
permits electronic occupation to change which nuclear sector
is energetically selected. [9][10]
The initial electronic Hilbert space is
H1 = L2(S2)⊗C2,
HNe =
Ne
�
H1.
The two components are declared spin labels. Antisymmetriza-
tion determines the available occupations. Helium retains ev-
ery charge sector Ne = 0,1,2; carbon retains Ne = 0,...,6. On
a sphere of radius R the one-electron generator is
hU = τ
R2 (−∆S2)− g
RU ·n,
H(q) = Enuc(q)+dΓ(hU(q)).
Here dΓ is the fermionic sum of the one-body action and n is
the position direction. The coefﬁcient τ controls kinetic action.
The same actual U(q) couples to every electron. Replacing
that common nuclear variable by independent electron aver-
ages would deﬁne another source.
The completed source calculations use the project’s classiﬁ-
cation: The test result suggests strong contact with the concept.
The physical charge, energy and length assignment is carried
by its own common-source recipient; it is not supplied by the
neutral count.

Rotational covariance reduces each conditional spectrum to
a function of |U|2, while the original nuclear weights stay at-

squared values. Across the declared parameter panel, exact
sharing yields 240 dimensionless spectral packets. At angular
preﬁx L = 32, each packet retains 1089 spatial modes and 2178
spin orbitals. The number of possible Ne-electron occupations
is a binomial count in that orbital inventory; the calculation
does not form a matrix of that binomial dimension.

XIV.4 Electronic occupation feeds back into the nuclear
minimum
The universal full-space electronic ground coefﬁcient eNe
obeys
−Ne/4 ≤ eNe ≤ cNe < 0.
The exact coupled nuclear selection is the lower envelope
min
q {Enuc(q)−tq},
t = (−eNe)g2/τ,
q = |U|2.
Every original nuclear sector participates in this envelope. The
completed calculation obtains eleven response phases and six
exact transition values. Helium cover 3 changes from q = 4 to
36 at t = 43/8; cover 4 changes from 16 to 36 at 129/5. Car-
bon 333 changes from 36 to 324 at 35/24; carbon 343 follows
64 → 256 → 324 at 35/24 and 123/17; carbon 444 changes
from 144 to 324 at 124/15. [10]
Combining the full electronic coefﬁcient bounds with this
exact nuclear envelope supplies forty-four nonempty coupling
ranges that guarantee a nuclear receiver phase for the whole-
space electron ground. The intermediate carbon 343 envelope
exists exactly; its six intermediate coupling guarantees are un-
resolved with those electronic bounds. This is a precise re-
maining assignment within an otherwise completed response
calculation. The envelope retains the nuclear energy price of
changing receiver, so a larger |U| is not selected freely.
This feature provides an important construction principle for
the rest of the volume. The source, recipient and occupied
state are solved together through a shared energy. A signed re-
ceiver is neither a ﬁxed empirical charge nor a coefﬁcient that
can be transferred independently of its nuclear energy. Sub-
sequent scalar and polarization ﬁelds preserve that distinction
while adding explicit interactions and ﬁnite core self energy.
XIV.5 One scalar and three polarization channels
The interacting atom uses the Green normalization G(x,y) =
1/(2π|x − y|) and a positive stored constrained-displacement
energy. A scalar channel assigns proton +1, neutron 0 and
electron −1. Three polarization channels retain the original
vector U and the electron direction n(x). The incidence is a
declared source construction. For a uniform nuclear shell of
radius rc, the resulting terms are

The coefﬁcients derive from the source amplitudes and ﬁeld

e /(2πκ). Equal scalar
proton/electron magnitudes give gc = vc. For vc ≥ vp ≥ 0 the
pair interaction is pointwise nonnegative. [11][12]
Core self energy stays inside both nuclear minima. The reg-
ularized electron self, Ne(vc + vp)/(2re), is common at ﬁxed
inventory; it cancels between coupled and separated prepara-
tions before taking the point-electron limit. A q-dependent
core self cannot be cancelled between different nuclear states.
This bookkeeping determines the full binding reference:
Esep = min
q [Enuc(q)+Score(q)],
Eatom = min
q [Enuc(q)+Score(q)+Ee,Ne(q)].
Binding is Esep −Eatom. Both use the same ﬁeld recipient and
intrinsic source.
The retained radial orbitals generate every product multipole
through rank 32. The polarization contraction also carries ad-
jacent radial orders through 33. Exact Gaunt coefﬁcients and
complete two-region radial integrals retain off-diagonal orbital
products and signed magnetic terms. These products deﬁne di-
rect and exchange entries on the actual physical Gram. The
calculation therefore includes the changing pair ﬁeld of the oc-
cupied state, with its own angular structure.
The complete retained fermion spaces use one, two and four
spatial orbitals with both spins. All conﬁgurations through the
actual helium and carbon inventories are retained. Grouping
by particle number, spin-up inventory and total magnetic num-
ber preserves every conﬁguration. The resulting 59,052 cer-
tiﬁed conﬁguration spectra include kinetic action, ﬁnite-core
attraction and full scalar/polarization pair terms. Their largest
block has twelve states. The original all-angular and all-radial
complement is covered separately by the whole-space lower
account.

SAM VOLUME II
One source, one energy convention: isotope tests and the physical map
Chapter XVI
XVI.1 The question after a complete construction
The completed constructions now supply more than a roster of
isotope labels. They retain legal internal states, occupied elec-
tronic sectors, ﬁeld squares, counterterms, recoil, and the free
systems against which formation is measured. This makes a
more speciﬁc physical question possible: can one source law
calculate the bound object and its free constituents in the same
energy convention, and then carry that law across isotopes?
The question connects the exact matter construction to the mea-
sured energy comparison. Its operands are the full systems de-
veloped in the preceding chapters.
Three recent records make the distinction concrete. The car-
bon isotope calculation transfers an already speciﬁed source
into a complete prepared-electron family. The light-isotope
calculation tests a particular scalar species-current law against
a held helium extension. The 37-isotope preparation lays out
a common prediction convention and preserves the structural
operands for the next source derivation. These records have
different completed outputs. Their relationship gives the vol-
ume a precise source-to-energy map.[65][66][67]
This chapter synthesizes the completed records.
Their
source hypotheses and numerical outcomes remain explicit.
The scalar species-current extension is a separate construction
from the original matter ledger.
XVI.2 The calculated atomic reference
For a neutral isotope with proton count Z and neutron count N,
the prepared common-law comparison is
Bat(Z,N) = ZEH1,free +NEn,free −Eatom(Z,N).
(XVI.1)
Each energy is calculated with the same source, electron, ﬁeld,
counterterm and energy-zero convention. A measured compos-
ite mass is a later comparator. The free endpoints are mathe-
matical recipients in the same construction as the atom. This
is particularly useful when a source contains a nonzero self-
energy or an identity baseline: the term has a deﬁnite place in
both sides of the subtraction.[67]
Common additive proton, neutron and electron rest base-
lines cancel once in Equation (XVI.1). The masses inside ki-
netic and collective recoil operators still affect the calculated
state. Their role is dynamical. An independently supplied in-
ertial mass must therefore remain listed among the inputs even
when a common rest-energy constant disappears from the for-
mation difference.
The neutral-atom convention also differs from bare-nuclear
binding by a speciﬁed electronic contribution:
Bat = Bnuclear +Belectrons(atom)−ZBelectrons(H1).
(XVI.2)
Here the electronic binding quantities are positive. A compar-
ison to a bare-nucleus reference needs this conversion and the
corresponding bounds. It cannot change the reference system
merely by changing the label on a table column. Fragment sep-
aration energies likewise come from differences of calculated
endpoints. The 37-isotope preparation explicitly excludes mea-
sured light-fragment bindings as ﬁxed offsets in the prediction
inputs.
The same principle applies to numerical enclosures. Sub-
tracting two variational upper witnesses does not establish an
enclosure for their difference. A binding interval needs com-
patible lower and upper information for the complete systems.
The source preparations retain whole-ground lower and upper
recipients, as well as the reference-dependent electronic and
recoil corrections needed when the nuclear source changes.
XVI.3 Units and source identiﬁcation are separate steps
The retained independent unit map is
Eunit = 2α2mec2,
(XVI.3)
Lunit =
¯hc
2αmec2 ,
(XVI.4)

¯h
Eunit
.
(XVI.5)
The declared inputs are the existing CODATA reference values
for the ﬁne structure constant, electron rest energy, Planck con-
stant, speed of light and elementary charge. These provide a
common dimensional chart without selecting parameters from
isotope binding values. The provenance of those constants re-
mains part of the calculation.[67]
A dimensional chart and a source assignment do different
work. The chart says how a quantity with an established role
is expressed in energy, length or time units. A source assign-
ment says which internal current acts on which ﬁeld, with what
spatial proﬁle, radius and coupling. The retained D/C source
and the electronic source have their own speciﬁed operators.
Placing both beside the same unit ruler does not determine
their intertwiner. That map is an explicit part of the continu-
ing connector-action question.
This separation explains why a dimensionless spectrum re-
mains valuable before all physical inputs have been selected. It
can determine sectors, degeneracies, conditional minima, op-
erator identities and source-dependent thresholds. A physical
prediction then uses the selected source law and the common
unit map.
The complete earlier spectrum is retained as an
operand, rather than recalculated under a new interpretation
of its units.

XVI.14 Independent nuclear discriminants and isotope
transfer
Identifying the pure, unextended U(4)-invariant scalar law
with the entire physical nuclear force would impose identical
spatial singlet and triplet S-wave operators at identical ﬂavor
kinetics. An independent neutron–proton analysis instead re-
ports triplet scattering length 5.4112(15) fm and singlet length
−23.7148(43) fm.
This discriminates that complete-force
identiﬁcation.
A scalar component supplemented by sepa-
rately speciﬁed spin/isospin interactions is a different receiv-
ing model whose terms and uncertainty must enter both energy
branches. [184]
Physical He4 + d → Li6 + γ also needs an electromagnetic
capture current and an angular-momentum/emission account.
The LUNA measurement of this radiative capture supplies an
independent experimental channel. The scalar cloud and the
auxiliary clock’s emitted energy are not calculations of that
electromagnetic process. [185]
The next isotope should therefore inherit a tested receiving
law, not the numerical gain of a different prepared source. Its
construction needs the actual Pauli metric and placement ge-
ometry, complete physical density images, full ﬂavor capaci-
ties, shared-ﬁeld correlations, and every proper fragment under
the same energy reference. Electronic occupation and recoil
then supply the atomic endpoints already deﬁned in this chap-
ter. The November results make this a sequence of speciﬁed
source and response calculations while preserving the com-
pleted He3 and Li6 certiﬁcates.

SAM VOLUME II
Carrier return and matter reception
Chapter XVII
XVII.1 How a closed object remains a source
Finite closure does not isolate a material object from the sub-
strate account. An admitted row continues to emit source sup-
port, and a composite must retain a rule for the support emitted
by its connected conﬁguration. The return route begins with
the admitted object and ends with the source accepted by the
accumulation ﬁeld. It preserves the object as matter through-
out the route.
The support decomposition established in Chapter I is
qA �−→
�
T = qA
8 ,W = 7qA
8
�
.
(XVII.1)
The next operation is ledger compression, schematically
Cledger(T,W,source address) �−→ rs.
(XVII.2)
Only after this operation does the spatial readout take the form
A(r) = rs
r .
(XVII.3)
The compression symbol identiﬁes the interface supplied by
the source construction. It is not an additional universal SI
conversion derived in this chapter. The ﬁeld readouts that con-
sume A(r) remain those of Volume I.[1, 2]
The order matters. Directly substituting qA for measured
mass would omit the carrier and compression stages. Calling
the released carrier another material constituent would instead
count a channel of support as an added object. Both short-
cuts change the source semantics even when they produce a
familiar-looking scalar expression.
The present chapter follows this route through functional
carrier and container roles, then examines the more detailed
ATOM3D reception constructions.
The early part concerns
exact ledger distinctions and scoped catalog tests. The later
part concerns ﬁnite information receipts, graph reconstruction
and candidate support demand. The two parts are related, but
neither supplies an automatic conversion from informational
structure to physical length or energy.
XVII.2 Conservation and the controls that identify its
scope
The source record QP092d reports conservation tests across
seven particle-source rows, six macro-ledger rows and eigh-
teen propagation rows.
All 31 scoped rows pass their re-
construction checks, and three ledger scopes close. The re-
ported maximum conservation relative error is approximately
2.98274132099 × 10−19. The same record adds zero matter
rows and rejects direct qA-as-mass use on 24 of 24 tested
rows.[3]
The coexistence of successful conservation and failed direct
mass identiﬁcation is instructive.
The exact decomposition
concerns the sum of two support channels. It says nothing
by itself about which downstream physical quantity should be
identiﬁed with that sum. A construction can preserve its source
quantity exactly while an attempted physical interpretation of
the quantity fails.
The LC03 replay follows the longer matter-to-accumulation
route and retains ﬁfteen rejected wrong controls. The alter-
natives include missing carrier support, altered fractions, pro-
motion of carriers to matter, vector/scalar substitution, mass-
only source, omitted compression and direct use of support as
measured mass. Each alternative changes a particular interface
rather than merely perturbing an arbitrary output.
Some of these distinctions can be seen without a numerical
test. Deleting T while retaining W leaves only 7qA/8, so the
original support is no longer conserved. Other alternatives re-
quire the source’s full typed structure. If both fractions were
changed so that their sum remained one, the generic arithmetic
conservation equation would still hold, but the speciﬁed one-
eighth carrier relation to the structural domain would no longer
be preserved. Conservation alone therefore does not select the
source’s fractions; the locked structural relation and its scoped
controls are also part of the construction.
The tests should be read at this resolution. A rejected wrong
fraction supports dependence on the speciﬁed domain relation.
Its result has the scope of the speciﬁed carrier-transport mech-
anism. A successful replay establishes the mechanism it actu-
ally reconstructs, using the inputs and gates it records.
XVII.3 Output ﬁxed point and terminal closure
Carrier and container roles are classiﬁed by source addresses,
not by sorting their magnitudes. In CR281 the addresses have

closure at address 162; ﬁxed-point behavior at (a,b) = (2,1)
or a = 0,b ≥ 3; output behavior at a = 1,1 ≤ b ≤ 3 or (a,b) =
(0,2); and OUT otherwise.[3]

dering, parity, pure versus mixed powers, an unrestricted grade,
swapped primitive addresses and inverted labels.

XVII.5 Cardinality closure without a geometric assign-
ment
From the completed relation W9 = 8 + 1 = 9, the source con-
structs the full-face value
F = 92 = 81.
(XVII.10)
Removing a non-row closure address leaves
F −1 = 80 = (9−1)(9+1) = 8·10.
(XVII.11)
The paired face ledger is L = 2F = 162. These relations or-
ganize a useful cardinality contact: eighty active rows and one
non-row address complete an eighty-one-position face.
Cardinality does not determine placement. CR283 tested
two speciﬁed placements: a punctured 9 × 9 row map and a
single-ﬁeld ten-octet grouping. Both precommitted searches
returned zero valid geometries. The result therefore has two
parts. The arithmetic contact remains exact; the proposed row-
to-face maps failed to supply the required geometry.[3]
There is no need to discard the cardinality because the map
failed, and no justiﬁcation for claiming the map because the
cardinality succeeded. A ﬁnite set can have the right number of
members for a target without satisfying the target’s adjacency,
class or incidence constraints. Those constraints are additional
mathematical content.
The N100-to-F81 audit makes the same distinction in a
computation-facing setting.
The reported source-native sur-
face has 100 unique N100 keys, 72 rows inside the exact
aggregate-incidence domain and an 81-site target with ten
classes. It supplies zero source-authorized elementwise assign-
ments; the contact lane is not executed. The resulting bound-
ary prevents the missing map from being ﬁlled by ﬁtting an
outcome. It also keeps an executable grammar from rewriting
the physical source tables it consumes.
XVII.6 From ledgers to an ATOM3D scaffold
The ATOM3D branch asks how typed matter information can
be represented as exact incidence and, in selected cases, as an
unscaled geometric readout. Its current authority distinguishes
an informational construction from a calibrated physical nu-
clear model. The distinction is maintained even when a graph
has a visually recognizable shape.[5]
The reported A1 O-16 scaffold installs eight identiﬁed pro-
tons, eight neutrons and eight electrons. It organizes 100 N100
roles into eight components, with 260 exact tensor relations,
eight octahedral sites and 48 directed reciprocal actions. Its
validation passes 14 of 14 checks. At that stage the scaffold
assigns neither matter endpoints nor physical coordinates. The
count of relations is a property of the installed ledger, not a
measurement of nuclear geometry.
The hydrogen ladder then makes increasingly structured ﬁ-
nite objects. H-1 is an empty-bridge control: one proton, no
neutron, no inter-nucleon pair and no carrier event. The re-
ported validation passes 22 of 22. H-2 installs the ﬁrst recip-
rocal proton-neutron connector and calibrates it at 2.2245662
MeV, with 29 of 29 validation checks. The energy here is a
calibration. It is not an unanchored prediction of the preceding
ﬁnite counts.
The H-3 and He-3 construction supplies reﬂected three-
role paths and a frozen common prediction of 4.4491324
MeV. It misses the two reference values by 4.03266380 and
3.26890892 MeV respectively. The source classiﬁcation is fal-
siﬁcation of that concept. The residual separates into a com-
mon part 3.65078636 MeV and a signed half-difference of

A later signed-surface join matches the centered sign of the

The sequence is useful because the successful structural
pieces and the failed energy transfer can coexist. Reﬂection
and exact incidence may be correctly implemented while a pro-
posed calibration transfer is wrong. Treating a passing valida-
tor as proof of the physical prediction would lose that distinc-
tion: the validator can conﬁrm that the frozen prediction was
calculated correctly, including when the prediction disagrees
with the measurement.

XVII.8 Correcting the carbon and balanced-relation types
The C12 type correction distinguishes three neutral seed cells
from a whole-object coordinate. Three seeds give 6p + 6n.
The aggregate quark coordinates are u = d = 18, matching
the scalar Theta value at the whole-object level. The correc-
tion does not establish a Theta multiplicity of three. The quan-
tity 54 = 3Θ remains an unassigned derived three-dimensional
quantity in that record.
This is another instance of the central type discipline. A
seed count and a whole-object coordinate can enter the same
construction without being interchangeable. Multiplying a fa-
miliar scalar by the number of seeds does not establish that
the object contains that many physical tensors. The corrected
workbench retains the neutral seeds and places Theta18 at the
whole-object level; the mistaken earlier artifacts remain pre-
served.
The balanced relation-emergence test reports closures

XVII.9 The accumulation carrier and graph reconstruc-
tion
AFC1 selects the separate A-ﬁeld carrier for the local accu-
mulation relay in sixteen of sixteen cases. This carrier is not
the non-row conjugation operator A. Maintaining the distinc-
tion prevents the accumulation ﬁeld, an operator on charged
antecedents and a transport role from being collapsed under
the same letter.
AFC2 reconstructs a 3p + 3n object from eighteen target-
barred records. Its degree sequence is 4,4,3,3,2,2, and its ag-
gregate quark coordinates are u = d = 9. Wrong-information
controls can preserve the aggregate nine while losing the ob-
ject. The result therefore tests more than a total: it relies on
relation identities, endpoints and the information needed to re-
construct the graph.
AFC3 applies classical multidimensional scaling to the
anonymous graph. The reported output has rank three, nonzero
tetrahedral volume and four symmetries. The reveal identiﬁes
one full matter exchange, a proper 180-degree rotation about
a common axis containing the three paired midpoints. Scram-
bled or disconnected ledgers fail the corresponding construc-
tion, while tampering and density-only alternatives change the
shape.[5]
The coordinates are unscaled. Rank three establishes the di-
mension of the reported geometric representation under the se-
lected construction, not a calibrated nuclear size. A proper ro-
tation in that representation is a mathematical symmetry of the
reconstructed object. It does not by itself identify a measured
motion, a binding potential or a dynamical law. The source ex-
plicitly leaves physical length, qA, binding and energy outside
this result.
The distinction between exact incidence and displayed coor-
dinates is useful. Incidence identiﬁes which relations exist. A
geometric embedding represents those relations using coordi-
nates under a speciﬁed method. Physical calibration would
be an additional map from that representation to measured
units and observables. Preserving the three stages makes the
ﬁnite reconstruction interpretable without overstating its phys-
ical reach.
XVII.10 Relative support and complete delivery
AFC4 joins the frozen relay and hologram ledger to the in-
stalled accumulation-conditioned support account.
Matter-
side qA remains source support, qA/8 is the candidate carrier-
support budget, and the A-ﬁeld carrier remains non-matter.
The reported gravity-only and velocity-only lanes agree when
they have the same total accumulation.
With the inherited A0 = 1/(12π), the normalized demand
is one baseline at A = A0, π baselines at A = 1/12, 11π base-
lines at A = 11/12, and 12π baselines at A = 1. The source
interprets the 11/12 condition as the end of reliable old-object
closure and the A = 1 condition as a parent-road reset within
that construction. These are inherited support hypotheses and
relative outputs, not a newly derived SI-energy law.[5]
The ﬁnite delivery test enumerates all 512 reciprocal-
delivery patterns of the nine-relation Li-6 ledger. Only com-
plete delivery reproduces the exact AFC3 shape. Every single-
relation loss changes it. Six of the nine single losses also de-
stroy the full proton-neutron exchange; three preserve that ex-
change while deforming the object. Two missing relations can
disconnect it. The reported independent validation passes 39
of 39.
This is a useful example of why both symmetry and object
identity must be checked. Preserving one exchange symmetry
does not guarantee preservation of the whole shape, and pre-
serving a total count does not guarantee the required graph.
Complete relation delivery supplies more information than
those compressed diagnostics. The result is a ﬁnite, scoped
statement about the reconstruction from its receipts.
It installs relative support demand, not joules and not a
ﬁnite bounce-to-energy operator.
The physical meaning of
the extreme accumulation regime remains separately bounded.
The chapter does not convert a successful information-delivery
reconstruction into an experimental demonstration of matter
transformation.

XVII.12 The return that can be carried forward
The ﬁnite return account is now explicit. An admitted ma-
terial conﬁguration emits typed support.
Its carrier and re-

supplies the interface to accumulation. Functional addresses
distinguish output, ﬁxed-point and terminal roles. Selected re-
ception tests reconstruct exact graphs, unscaled geometric rep-
resentations and relative support behavior from ﬁnite receipts.
The account preserves the failures that deﬁne its reach: no
direct support-as-mass identity, no carrier promotion through a
numerical lift, no duplicate operator row, no successful CR283
row-to-face geometry, no universal calibrated energy transfer,
and no automatic physical interpretation of informational ten-
sors. These limits do not remove the exact constructions. They
identify the information that may be exported without alter-
ation to the computation volume.

XVIII.1 A ﬁnite object carries more than its present read-
out
Finite closure gives Volume II an object that can maintain a
state. Interaction adds a second question: what information
from the construction remains available to its next receiver? A
scalar support account, a current, an ordered source history and
a quantum ﬁeld state answer different parts of that question.
The completed matter constructions now connect these parts
explicitly. They begin with actual source phase coordinates,
identify the information removed by a current projection, con-
struct a contact and carrier on the retained receiver, and carry
their actions into a full outgoing quantum ﬁeld. This supplies
a substantial development beyond a catalog of admitted rows
or an arithmetic carrier budget.[29]
The distinction can ﬁrst be seen in a small exact example
developed in the completed retained-history manuscript. The
legal connector histories (1,8) and (8,1) have the same termi-
nal sum, 9. With the source lift
L(p) = p+ p2
144,
(XVIII.1)
the same permitted ﬁrst-preﬁx readout gives 145/144 and
76/9, respectively, separated by 119/16.
Their symmetric
full-contact residual is instead 1/9 in both cases. The infor-
mation needed depends on the receiver’s question. The termi-
nal sum is sufﬁcient for summation; the ordered operands are
required for the ﬁrst-preﬁx action. Those response values re-
tain their source meanings, without acquiring a physical energy
unit through the calculation.[30]
This example gives a precise meaning to equality of present
readouts. If π exports an observation and C is a subsequent
admitted action, then
π(x) = π(y),
C(x) ̸= C(y)
(XVIII.2)
means that C needs information beyond π. The completed
matter results supply this pattern inside ﬁxed isotope inven-
tories.
Their hidden information consists of original phase
coordinates and actual ﬁeld records.
It enters later source
responses through explicit maps.
Consequently, directional
history has a ﬁnite material meaning: it can determine how
the same next interaction acts on two presently equal receiver
preparations.[30][31]
The following construction keeps three related accomplish-
ments distinct. A reciprocal event can be packed into one com-
pleted response record. An actual matter contact and carrier
can generate the whole response algebra on six source coordi-
nates. A full source can be encoded into native phase records
while retaining every source direction. Each achievement has
its own operands and equality.
Their composition is much
more informative than identifying objects from a shared inte-
ger such as 18 or a shared matrix dimension.[31][32][33]
