XVIII.9 The material interface supplied by these results
The completed chain now has a clear material interpretation.
Source phases determine currents; a present current can omit
preparation that affects the next contact. Reciprocal event com-
pletion preserves an exact receiver response. A literal contact
and energy-preserving carrier generate the full algebra on ac-
tual relative currents. The edge ﬁeld receives those directions
through a dual metric. Finite-time normal wave evolution car-
ries older increments and ordered phases after local contact
means agree. Finally, the paired native phase chart retains the
full original source and transports the whole quantum recipient
without tracing away its reference factors.[31][32][35][36][37]
These results extend the ﬁnite-matter foundation in the lan-
guage of source, event, receiver and retained history. They also
locate the remaining physical assignments precisely. The his-
torical W9P/Theta identity remains a source-comparison ques-
tion; carrier evolution frequency, autonomous output ampli-
tudes and physical incidence belong to their corresponding
dynamical recipients. The common energy, action and ruler
map must carry the same retained source into binding and
atomic or molecular comparisons. Each completed report re-
tains its own source-era boundary, while later chapters develop
the subsequent energetic and occupied-matter recipients. The
exact maps presented here provide the common object through
which those developments can be composed.[32][33][35]
XVIII.10 Results received, 25 October 2026
The phase source of this chapter was reproduced natively from
its archive operands on 25 October 2026 (ironclad H002783;
Chapter XII, Section XII.9). What the reproduction adds to the
chapter is the name of the retained object: the two full phase
records are one frame under the complex structure J, and the
information a current-based readout drops is the J-antilinear
part of each placement tensor, carried by a Z4 register with a
conserved label and a gauge. “A ﬁnite object carries more than
its present readout” (Section XVIII.1) is therefore exact in this
sense: the present readout is the J-linear part, and the comple-
ment is a register, not a payment. Volume I’s inertia identity
and the spin engine’s hidden stage are different mechanisms
with the same symptom, and the three are listed side by side,
not equated, in the editorial spine of the volumes.

SAM VOLUME II
The ﬁnite handoff to computation
Chapter XIX
XIX.1 What leaves the matter volume
The matter volume ends with objects that a computation can
receive without changing their scientiﬁc status. They include
typed particle rows, native generator forms, admission and sta-
bility ﬁelds, mass-lift channels, nuclear population ledgers, ele-
ment and isotope addresses, carrier budgets, closure addresses
and selected relation receipts. Some are exact algebraic con-
structions; some are catalog records; some carry measured
comparators or ﬁtted parameters. A faithful computation pre-
serves those distinctions.
The receiving volume develops the Substrate Ledger Com-
puter and its language, exact-state representations, write lifecy-
cle and applications. It does not need to repeat every physical
argument in order to serialize a matter row. It does need to
know what the row means, which operations are deﬁned on it
and which results remain only conditional or empirical. The
handoff is therefore a contract between types and operators,
rather than a claim that execution supplies missing physical
evidence.[1, 2]
The conceptual route is ﬁnite matter grammar, typed mass
and binding channels, carrier/container return, computation
grammar, language and write lifecycle. This route connects
the volumes while leaving ownership of each result clear. Mat-
ter deﬁnitions and comparisons remain in Volume II. Runtime
behavior and computational exploitation belong to Volume III.
A successful execution may validate its implementation of the
received rule; it does not by itself validate the rule as a descrip-
tion of nature.
XIX.2 The row as a serializable scientiﬁc object
The row identity introduced in Chapter I contains a source ta-
ble, schema, address and acting operator. A computational rep-
resentation must retain enough of that identity to distinguish
rows whose numerical ﬁelds happen to agree. It must also re-
tain the difference between an input and a derived ﬁeld.
For example, a native value, a debit and a resulting readout
are three related ﬁelds. If the representation stores only the
readout, a null result could be indistinguishable from a row that
never had a native candidate. The LC06 witness shows why the
history matters: native value 18 and debit 18 produce a null
readout, and that cancellation has a different meaning from an
absent record. Exact arithmetic can preserve the cancellation
only if the operands and their types remain available.
Likewise, a known particle or element label should remain a
reveal ﬁeld. Moving the label into the address-generation stage
would alter the inference order even if the displayed table re-
mained unchanged. The computational interface must preserve
which information was available at construction, which was
used for selection and which was opened only for comparison.
The same applies to admission. A nonzero native value is
not a replacement for the admission predicate. A rejected can-
didate can be represented exactly and can remain valuable as a
control. A hidden-support row can be needed for the grammar
while being barred from matter-source emission. The receiv-
ing language should be able to carry those statuses without
turning every stored record into an admitted physical object.
This is not a new implementation speciﬁcation. It states the
semantic content of the source objects already used in the vol-
ume. Different implementations may encode the ﬁelds differ-
ently, but they cannot omit a load-bearing distinction and still
claim to reproduce the same scientiﬁc object.
XIX.3 Exact rationals before decimal display
Several central quantities are rational:
1
8,
5
48,
17
192,
(XIX.1)
κ = 7117

7093
192 .
(XIX.2)
The exact forms support cancellation and comparison without
introducing a display tolerance into the underlying identity. A
decimal representation is useful for communicating scale, but
it should not replace the rational source when the claim con-
cerns exact equality.
The normalized-square asymmetry relation is an example.
Its coefﬁcient follows from the exact difference between two
source channels. Storing a rounded decimal for each channel
and subtracting them can introduce a residual that belongs to
the representation rather than the model. Retaining the rational
forms makes the source of exactness explicit and separates it
from the precision used for later measured comparisons.
Exact representation does not remove the need for units. The
rational 7117/768 is a structural coefﬁcient in the declared
channel. A measurement in MeV or atomic mass units enters
through its own conversion and comparison speciﬁcation. Ex-
actness of the rational coefﬁcient does not turn that conversion
into a derived physical law.
Undeﬁned expressions require equal care. The K = X = 0
carrier row remains part of the lift table while Y = X/K is
undeﬁned. A receiving program that ﬁlls the quotient with
zero would preserve the table’s length but change its mathe-
matical content. Filling it with one would impose a different
unsupported convention. The appropriate representation distin-
guishes a retained row with an inapplicable or undeﬁned quo-
tient from an ordinary numerical value.
This distinction also affects aggregate statistics. A reported
closure of 138 evaluable quotients is not a failure to account
for a 139-row table. It is a statement that one row lies outside
the quotient’s domain. A computation that silently includes an
invented endpoint could appear more complete while actually
weakening the speciﬁcation.

SAM Volume II | Chapter XIX

XIX.4 Source inventories and executable grammars
The executable N100 basis contains one hundred typed com-
putation rows. It is not a replacement census for the physical-
source surfaces. The matter volume has already used 35 com-
pact mass rows, 139 lift-table rows, 321 catalog rows, 126 in-
ventory rows, an 80-row stable structural surface and a 126-
row periodic surface. Their counts remain attached to their
own selection predicates.
A computation may prune or select a basis for an operation.
The cross-volume source gives the example of removing ﬁve
p:9 rows from an executable basis. Such a selection does not
delete rows from the particle catalog, the mass table or the pe-
riodic source. It changes the operation’s admitted domain. The
source inventories must remain recoverable without applying
the computation’s pruning as a scientiﬁc revision.
This is analogous to the distinction between a table and a
query over the table, but the consequences here are mathemat-
ical. An executable basis can omit a source row because the
chosen operator is not deﬁned on it, because it is redundant
for a particular representation, or because the operation uses
a narrower class. None of those possibilities is equivalent to
declaring that the source row is physically nonexistent or ex-
perimentally rejected.
A program can therefore consume a 126-row periodic input
while using a 100-row operation grammar. It can compare a
139-row lift table while evaluating only 138 quotients. It can
preserve a 321-row catalog that includes carriers and rejected
candidates while selecting a smaller matter-allowed subset for
source support. Correctness requires the selection rule and its
domain, not equality of all counts.
The source-authorized N100-to-F81 elementwise join re-
mains absent in the inspected record. Its aggregate contact is
not enough to ﬁll the missing assignment. A receiving compu-
tation can carry both source and target objects and can record
that the join is undeﬁned; it need not invent the map to remain
a complete representation of the current state.
XIX.5 Writes receipts and retained history
The W8/X1/W9 lifecycle supplies a computation-facing lan-
guage for a supported relation, activation and completion. In
the current typed reading, these roles are distinct from ho-
mogeneous mass-lift tensors.
Theta18 supplies a carrier or
completion-report role at the relevant interface, while the A-
ﬁeld carrier supplies transport.
The matter volume exports
these meanings rather than merely the displayed values 8, 1,
9 and 18.
The reception results show why an endpoint value can be
an insufﬁcient record. Different relation histories or incom-
plete delivery patterns can preserve an aggregate number while
changing the reconstructed object. A computation that retains
only the ﬁnal count would lose the information needed to dis-
tinguish those cases. Exact receipts preserve relation identity,
direction, address and payload at the scope established by the
source test.
The AFC2 and AFC3 chain is a concrete example. A scalar
coordinate such as u = d = 9 does not identify the six-object
incidence structure. The eighteen target-barred records carry
information that the aggregate omits.
The geometry is re-
constructed from that information under a declared procedure.
The subsequent delivery test shows that a retained symmetry
can survive some losses even though the exact shape changes.
A successful serialization must therefore preserve more than
the diagnostic that happens to remain constant.
The relation between an informational receipt and a physi-
cal event remains a separate interpretive question. An exact
reversible record can demonstrate ﬁdelity within a ﬁnite com-
putational model. It does not automatically establish a phys-
ical energy, a measured length or a causal matter-generation
process. Keeping the receipt exact is nevertheless valuable: it
preserves the construction so that its actual content can be in-
spected without relying on a picture or a compressed summary.
XIX.6 Empirical ﬁelds retain their provenance
The matter account includes several kinds of empirical con-
tact. A neutrino absolute scale uses a measured splitting. A
historical nuclear readout uses ﬁtted coefﬁcients and then eval-
uates a frozen extension. B5T identiﬁes an informational ten-
sor before binding targets are opened, then evaluates a binding
residual lane with its own development and held data. A target-
visible He-4 discovery calculation uses information that a blind
conﬁrmation would withhold.
These histories remain part of the exported object. A nu-
merical prediction cannot be interpreted solely from its ﬁnal
residual. The receiving program or report must preserve which
quantities were ﬁxed, which were ﬁt, which targets were vis-
ible, which rows were held and which controls were part of
the declared test. The same residual can mean different things
under different information histories.
A useful example is the phrase “zero reﬁt.”
For CR277
it identiﬁes the extension stage: the earlier ﬁtted operator is
frozen and applied to a broader representative table. It does
not say that the original coefﬁcients were never ﬁt. Similarly,
a typed-rational coefﬁcient selected near a ﬁtted value retains
that selection history even when the ﬁnal displayed coefﬁcient
is exact. Exact arithmetic and empirical selection are compat-
ible, but their combination is not the same as a coefﬁcient de-
rived without data contact.
The B5T target-blind tensor stage also must be stated pre-
cisely. Freezing the assignment before binding is opened pre-
vents binding from selecting that assignment or breaking its
tie. It does not mean that every subsequent component of the
binding prediction is unsupervised or that the measured scale
is derived from a ﬁnite permutation alone. The representation
and the residual test have related but distinct evidence burdens.
An executable record is most useful when it preserves these
distinctions rather than replacing them with a single PASS ﬂag.
The ﬂag identiﬁes the outcome of declared predicates. The
predicates, inputs, controls and classiﬁcation explain what that
outcome means.
XIX.7 Failures are part of the ﬁnite export
Several unsuccessful propositions are load-bearing parts of
this volume. The direct source-to-rest-mass identity failed its
locked control. CR241 retained low holdout residual while
100

SAM Volume II | Chapter XIX
Brady | 10 November 2026
failing candidate uniqueness. The proposed linear constituent
binding account did not supply the nonlinear binding read-
out.
The two CR283 geometry searches returned no valid
maps. Frozen hydrogen-ladder energy transfers missed their
measured targets. Target-visible reﬁnements remain discover-
ies rather than conﬁrmations.
A computational export that retains only successful rows
would make the source record less informative. The failures
distinguish quantities that would otherwise be easy to merge.
The direct mass failure separates coupling from rest-source
information. The failed geometry searches separate cardinal-
ity from placement. The energy-transfer misses separate exact
structural graphs from a calibrated physical law.
Failures also need their scope. A failed speciﬁc map has the
scope of the tested mapping. A failed frozen energy expres-
sion does not invalidate every relation in the graph on which it
was evaluated. Conversely, a surviving graph or exact identity
does not rescue the rejected physical prediction. The export
must retain the proposition tested, not merely a favorable or
unfavorable label.
This principle avoids two opposite distortions. One would
erase useful exact work whenever a stronger interpretation
fails. The other would use useful exact work to imply that
the stronger interpretation succeeded. The ﬁnite record sup-
ports neither shortcut. It permits each result to retain the status
earned by its own evidence.
XIX.8 Source custody for the completed account
The continuous matter synthesis in SAMA supplies the histor-
ical narrative foundations. The expanded chapters receive the
later completed source reports, derivations and current author-
ity summarized in the source register. Its frozen repository
version preserves the source chapters and the copied Court-
room material.
The SAM Research Project technical spine
supplies the source-bound recovery map, while its live binding
and ATOM3D documents control the current status of those
branches. The evidence-rich unapproved review assembly sup-
plies focused derivation and deviation chains without confer-
ring author approval by its existence.[1, 2, 3, 4, 5]
The sources have different authority roles. A later prose as-
sembly does not automatically supersede an executed result. A
repository reorganization does not change a scientiﬁc classiﬁ-
cation. A historical result remains evidence for its own test
even when a later domain authority supplies the current syn-
thesis. Where a source identiﬁes a missing permanent test key,
the missing key remains a custody boundary rather than being
replaced by a similar identiﬁer.
The numerical receipts quoted in this book are reported from
those records unless the text explicitly describes code inspec-
tion. The preparation of this manuscript did not rerun scien-
tiﬁc campaigns, reﬁt coefﬁcients or reproduce every indepen-
dent validator. Selected code inspection establishes what the
inspected routine does, such as retaining a ﬁtted coefﬁcient
when a typed substitution falls outside its threshold. It does
not substitute for execution of every dependency or indepen-
dent replication of the entire research program.
This distinction does not reduce a source-based book to a
mere list of claims. The exact algebra can be followed directly,
the deﬁnitions can be stated without ambiguity, and the histor-
ical tests can be described with their controls and limitations.
The resulting account is complete at the level it declares: an or-
ganized mathematical construction and its recorded evidence,
with physical identiﬁcations bounded by the evidence actually
available.
XIX.9 The matter account at its stopping point
The dedicated spin section adds exact joint state counting, con-
trolled thermal ordering, microscopic memory, composition
cancellation, source-speciﬁc kinetic clocks and the latest paid
physical response to the matter construction. These pass to
Volume III as source-bound exact factors, generators, certiﬁed
matrix operands and retained dependency closures.
The work developed here has a deﬁnite ﬁnite content. It
supplies a typed distinction between substrate support and ad-
mitted matter; complementary carrier and retained channels;
several explicitly different particle and matter surfaces; class-
dependent generator and lift laws; conditional Higgs and neu-
trino packets; a four-slot nuclear ledger; an exact channel-
gap asymmetry relation; historical ﬁtted and later held bind-
ing readouts; periodic-family and isotope organization; ﬁnite
reception and reconstruction results; complete conditional nu-
clear and thermal sources; causal ordered contact histories; oc-
cupied Pauli atoms; full-motion molecular recipients; isotope
recoil transfer; a held light-source test; and an explicit common
physical energy convention.
It also has deﬁnite limits. Candidate uniqueness does not
follow from a small residual. The absolute neutrino scale re-
tains its external anchor. A periodic frontier identity is not
an observation. The completed atomic and molecular recipi-
ents retain their declared source and prepared-family scopes.
An informational tensor or unscaled graph embedding is not
yet a calibrated physical nuclear law. The later chapters iden-
tify the achieved source, ﬁeld, occupied-state and outgoing-
record maps, together with the speciﬁc physical assignments
still open. These statements are the boundary of the present
account, not a schedule of obligations appended to it.
Volume III receives the objects on the established side of
that boundary together with the statuses that protect their mean-
ing. It can preserve them, transform them under declared oper-
ators and examine their computational consequences. It need
not solve every open physical question before doing so. The
ﬁnite handoff is thus the appropriate end of the matter volume:
exact structure where the construction is exact, recorded com-
parison where the evidence is empirical, and explicit limits
where the source has not completed the bridge.

A.5 Local baths and retained information
The spectral reset is an added many-body receiving law. A
candidate based on local physical densities and bounded ﬁeld
Weyl operators has the desired Gibbs stationary states and full
ﬂavor ﬁxed algebra when its interacting Bohr components and
thermal rates are supplied. A ﬁnite energy-relaxation rate and
a microscopic realization of those components remain open.

Brady | 10 November 2026
Ordinary bare D[b] loss does not implement interacting Gibbs
cooling. Freezing matter while cooling only the ﬁeld also pre-
serves every density probability and cannot create the required
orbital rearrangement.
Likewise, swapping in an already prepared interacting
Gibbs copy transfers the resource whose formation was at is-
sue; its preparation and reset must be charged. In every dis-
sipative implementation the full system-bath isometry can re-
tain source information that the reduced Gibbs state loses. The
bath outputs, failed arrivals, work-store correlations and source
replacement records are therefore part of the same formation
account. These conditional families specify concrete tests of
rate, response, information and energy for a subsequent physi-
cal implementation.

SAM VOLUME II
Evidence and scope of the November revision
Appendix B
This edition preserves the 5 November manuscript as its
baseline and receives the completed 8–10 November source,
veriﬁcation and formation records into Chapters XI, XII and
XVI, with a revised historical bridge and table layout in Chap-
ter V. Existing atomic, molecular, spin and historical binding
results keep their source laws and stated comparisons. The re-
vision is a manuscript synthesis; no scientiﬁc campaign was
rerun to prepare it.
B.1 Veriﬁed inputs and later constructions
The He3 shared complete-payment statement held in a fresh
derivation and new exact-rational veriﬁer. Its 285 recorded
checks and an isolated replay passed; the four sufﬁcient inter-
vals retain the full gate and sharp-moment premises identiﬁed
in Section XI.10. A recovered-matrix supplement corrects an
earlier extraction that stored four output labels instead of their
matrices. The original raw results and numerical claim were
unchanged.[164]
The free-reference case separately held its complete-parent
and preparation account with 247 exact checks; the shared-
ﬁeld covariance case held its ordered full-source payment with
108. Both retain internal I1/R2 veriﬁcation and their stated ge-
ometric/proﬁle domains. [166][167]
The Li6 case held all nine submitted claims:
the com-
mon full-Fock law, noncommuting fragment factors, univer-
sal proper-channel lower, physical hopping trial, ﬁnite actual-
source transfer, its channel margin, actual ﬂavor decomposi-
tion, ﬂavor-preserving map and unchanged-preparation energy
certiﬁcate. Three separately authored proof/code tracks sup-
plied nineteen controls and matched relocated replays. The re-
tained lookup and coordinate-order errors were corrected with-
out changing the claims. Both cases record internal I1/R2 ver-
iﬁcation, not external human review; their historical approval
ﬁelds remain as recorded.[172]
The later receiving-action results have analytical derivations,
internal peer review, native ﬁnite calculations and a lead consis-
tency check. That check rebuilt the eleven-state density Grams,
source norm and design rank, legal current-dark pair, spatial
weight inequalities and exact work, timing and calibration al-
lowances. It did not supply a new independent full-ﬂavor re-
construction of the arrival instrument or a physical realization
of the controllers. The 15/17 instrument, loading interfaces,
response design and longer reservoir/thermal constructions re-
tain this status throughout the book.[176]

B.3 Common energy reference
The original positive incidence quadratic, the complete-square
He3 ﬁeld and the Li6 linear-density scalar ﬁeld are separately
speciﬁed laws. The negative density term in the latter follows
from its full square completion and the absence of a compen-
sating density counterterm. A change of law must propagate
through both the joined upper and every fragment lower. The
receiving-action family gives examples of this propagation, in-
cluding a changed dynamics with the same static kernel.
The ﬁnite actual-source margin at g2 = 33/2 uses the
79τ/10 lower, whereas the unchanged14336 result at g2 = 35
uses the 8τ lower. The energy gains are not combined. The sep-
arate calibration, ﬁnite-time loading, battery/clock and thermal
margins have different error hypotheses; none is a measured
binding value. Electronic and atomic endpoints remain under
the common reference deﬁned in Chapter XVI.
